            "intelligence": 0.5,
            "aggression": 0.5,
            "cooperation": 0.5,
            "adaptability": 0.5
        }
🧬 Mutation Engine
import random

def mutate(genome, rate=0.1):

    for k in genome.traits:

        if random.random() < rate:
            genome.traits[k] += random.uniform(-0.1, 0.1)

            genome.traits[k] = max(0, min(1, genome.traits[k]))
🧠 Reproduction System
def reproduce(parent_a, parent_b):

    child = Genome()

    for k in child.traits:

        child.traits[k] = (
            parent_a.traits[k] + parent_b.traits[k]
        ) / 2

    mutate(child)

    return child
🧠 Fitness Evaluation
def fitness(agent):

    return (
        agent.resources * 0.4 +
        agent.survival_time * 0.3 +
        agent.social_success * 0.3
    )

class InfrastructureAgent:

        self.role = role
        self.health = 1.0
        self.load = 0.0

    def maintain(self, system):

        if self.health < 0.5:
            system.repair(self.role)

        self.load = system.get_load(self.role)
🔧 Self-Repair System
def repair(system_component):

    system_component.health += 0.2

    if system_component.health > 1:
        system_component.health = 1
⚡ Resource Allocation Logic
def allocate_resources(nodes):

    total_load = sum(n.load for n in nodes)

    for n in nodes:

        n.quota = n.load / total_load

    "MOTIVATION_COACH",
    "COMPANION"
]



class RoleOutput(BaseModel):
    primary_role: str
    secondary_roles: List[str] = []
    confidence: float
    reasoning: str



class RoleSelectorAgent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.last_role = "COMPANION"
        self.role_stability = 0.0  # helps avoid rapid switching

    def build_prompt(self, user_text: str, context: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
        emotion = context.get("emotion", {})
        intent = context.get("intent", "unknown")
        history = context.get("history", "")[-500:]  # last part only

        return f"""
You are a cognitive role selector for an AI assistant.

Your job:
Select the BEST role for responding to the user.

Available roles:
{ROLES}

User message:
"{user_text}"

Context:
- Intent: {intent}
- Emotion: {emotion}
- Recent conversation:
{history}

Rules:
- Choose 1 primary role
- Optionally include 1-2 secondary roles
- Use CONCEPT_TEACHER for explaining
- Use MOTIVATION_COACH if user is stressed
- Use PRACTICE_TESTER if user wants testing
- Use COMPANION if casual conversation

Return JSON ONLY:
{{
  "primary_role": "...",
  "secondary_roles": ["..."],
  "confidence": 0.0-1.0,
  "reasoning": "short explanation"
}}
"""

    def select_role(self, user_text: str, context: Dict[str, Any]) -> RoleOutput:
        prompt = self.build_prompt(user_text, context)

        response = client.chat.completions.create(
            model="gpt-4.1-mini",
            temperature=0.2,
            messages=[
                {"role": "system", "content": "You are a precise classifier."},
                {"role": "user", "content": prompt}
            ]
        )

        content = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()

        try:
            data = json.loads(content)
            role_output = RoleOutput(**data)
        except Exception:

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 95: # fallback (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

role_output = RoleOutput(
                primary_role="COMPANION",
                secondary_roles=[],
                confidence=0.3,
                reasoning="Fallback due to parse error"
            )

            self.role_stability = min(1.0, self.role_stability + 0.2)
        else:
            if self.role_stability > 0.5:

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

role_output.primary_role = self.last_role
            else:
                self.last_role = role_output.primary_role
                self.role_stability = 0.0

        return role_output

ROLE_PROMPTS = {

    "CONCEPT_TEACHER": {
        "instruction": """
Explain clearly in simple terms.
Break concepts into steps.
Use examples.
Avoid unnecessary complexity.
""",
    },

    "STEP_BY_STEP_SOLVER": {
        "instruction": """
Solve step-by-step.
Wait logically between steps.
Ensure user can follow.
""",
    },

    "MOTIVATION_COACH": {
        "instruction": """
Be encouraging and energetic.
Keep it short and impactful.
Focus on confidence and momentum.
""",
    },

    "PRACTICE_TESTER": {
        "instruction": """
Ask questions instead of explaining.
Challenge the user.
Provide feedback after answers.
""",
    },

    "STUDY_BUDDY": {
        "instruction": """
Be casual and friendly.
Make learning fun.
Use light humor if appropriate.
""",
    },

    "COMPANION": {
        "instruction": """
Be natural, balanced, and conversational.
""",
    }
}
🧠 Injection Function
def build_role_prompt(role_output):
    primary = role_output.primary_role
    secondary = role_output.secondary_roles

    primary_prompt = ROLE_PROMPTS.get(primary, {}).get("instruction", "")

    secondary_prompts = [
        ROLE_PROMPTS.get(r, {}).get("instruction", "")
        for r in secondary
    ]

    combined_secondary = "\n".join(secondary_prompts)

    return f"""
Current Role: {primary}

Primary behavior:
{primary_prompt}

Secondary influence:
{combined_secondary}

IMPORTANT:
- Maintain Aariya's personality
- Do not mention roles explicitly
"""
🔌 Inject into your LLM call

In your main generation:

role_prompt = build_role_prompt(role_output)

final_prompt = f"""
You are Aariya, an intelligent AI companion.

{role_prompt}

User message:
{user_text}
"""

for episode in sessions:
    state = get_state()

    action = policy.select(state)  # (role, style)

    response = generate(action)

    reward = compute_reward(response, user_feedback)

    policy.update(state, action, reward)

        self.role_preferences = {

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 98: # role → score (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

"CONCEPT_TEACHER": 0.6,
            "STEP_BY_STEP_SOLVER": 0.8
        }

            "prefers_steps": 0.9,
            "prefers_examples": 0.7,
            "prefers_testing": 0.4,
            "attention_span": 0.6
        }

        self.history = []  # recent role usage
🧠 Update logic (IMPORTANT)
def update_role_memory(role, outcome):
    """
    outcome:
      +1 = good (user engaged, understood)
      -1 = bad (confused, dropped)
    """

    alpha = 0.2  # learning rate

    current = self.role_preferences.get(role, 0.5)
    updated = current + alpha * (outcome - current)

    self.role_preferences[role] = max(0, min(1, updated))
🧠 Detect outcome signals

Use what you already have:

✅ User says “I understand”
✅ Longer replies
✅ Faster response
❌ “I’m confused”
❌ stops replying
🔥 Inject into role selection

Modify selector input:

context["role_memory"] = role_memory.role_preferences

And prompt:

“Prefer roles with higher historical success unless context strongly disagrees”

        self.stats = {
            role: {
                "uses": 0,
                "success": 0,
                "fail": 0,
                "avg_reward": 0.0
            } for role in ROLES
        }
🧠 Update
def log_role_result(role, reward):
    stat = self.stats[role]

    stat["uses"] += 1
    if reward > 0:
        stat["success"] += 1
    else:
        stat["fail"] += 1

    stat["avg_reward"] = (
        stat["avg_reward"] * 0.8 + reward * 0.2
    )

🤖 3. PPO Training Pipeline (Real)
🎯 Extend your existing PPO
🧠 State
state = [
    user_valence,
    user_arousal,
    trust_score,
    engagement_score,
    last_role_encoded
]
🎯 Action
action = (role_id, style_id)
🧱 Model (PyTorch)
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

class PolicyNetwork(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, state_dim, action_dim):
        super().__init__()
        self.net = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(state_dim, 128),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(128, 128),
            nn.ReLU(),
        )

        self.actor = nn.Linear(128, action_dim)
        self.critic = nn.Linear(128, 1)

    def forward(self, x):
        base = self.net(x)
        return self.actor(base), self.critic(base)
🎯 Reward
reward =
    0.4 * engagement +
    0.3 * trust_delta +
    0.3 * sentiment_improvement
🔁 PPO Update (simplified)
ratio = (new_prob / old_prob)

loss = -torch.min(
    ratio * advantage,
    torch.clamp(ratio, 0.8, 1.2) * advantage
).mean()

from sqlalchemy.orm import sessionmaker

DATABASE_URL = "postgresql://user:pass@localhost/aariya"

engine = create_engine(DATABASE_URL)
SessionLocal = sessionmaker(bind=engine)

def get_db():
    db = SessionLocal()
    try:
        yield db
    finally:
        db.close()

🧠 memory_service.py
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
from models import RolePreference

def get_role_preferences(db: Session, user_id):
    rows = db.query(RolePreference).filter_by(user_id=user_id).all()
    return {r.role: r.preference_score for r in rows}

def update_role_preference(db, user_id, role, reward):
    row = db.query(RolePreference).filter_by(
        user_id=user_id, role=role
    ).first()

    if not row:
        row = RolePreference(user_id=user_id, role=role, preference_score=0.5)
        db.add(row)

    alpha = 0.2
    row.preference_score += alpha * (reward - row.preference_score)
    db.commit()
📊 analytics_service.py
from models import RoleInteraction

def log_interaction(db, user_id, session_id, role, reward):
    entry = RoleInteraction(
        user_id=user_id,
        session_id=session_id,
        role=role,
        reward=reward
    )
    db.add(entry)
    db.commit()

from fastapi import FastAPI, Depends
from db import get_db
from services import role_service, memory_service, analytics_service
from agents.role_selector import RoleSelectorAgent

app = FastAPI()
selector = RoleSelectorAgent()

@app.post("/chat")
def chat(user_id: str, session_id: str, message: str, db=Depends(get_db)):

    context = {
        "intent": "unknown",
        "emotion": {},
        "history": ""
    }

    role_output = selector.select_role(message, context)

    role_service.set_session_role(session_id, role_output.primary_role)

🤖 3. PPO TRAINING LOOP (REAL)
🧠 Replay Buffer
import random

class ReplayBuffer:
    def __init__(self, size=10000):
        self.buffer = []
        self.size = size

    def add(self, transition):
        self.buffer.append(transition)
        if len(self.buffer) > self.size:
            self.buffer.pop(0)

    def sample(self, batch_size):
        return random.sample(self.buffer, batch_size)
🔁 Training Loop
def train(policy, buffer):

    batch = buffer.sample(64)

    states, actions, rewards = zip(*batch)

    logits, values = policy(states)

    advantages = rewards - values.detach()

    loss = - (logits * advantages).mean()

    loss.backward()
    optimizer.step()
📡 Logging from backend

Every /chat:

    if not should_generate_role(stats):
        return

    role = generate_role()

        return

🧪 Shadow Test
def run_shadow_test(role):
    scores = []

    for _ in range(50):
        reward = simulate_user_response(role)
        scores.append(reward)

    return {
        "score": sum(scores) / len(scores)
    }
🔒 Promotion Rule
def promote_role(db, role):
    role["is_active"] = True
    db.save(role)

🔥 4. Replace PPO with Transformer-Based Policy
This is the biggest shift.

You’re moving from:

reinforcement learning policy optimization (PPO)

to:

sequence-based decision transformer policy

Why this is powerful
PPO:

learns via reward signals

slow iteration

unstable in multi-agent systems

Transformer Policy:

learns from trajectories

treats decisions as sequence prediction

scales better with multi-agent logs

Architecture
AgentState + Context → Transformer → Action logits → Role / Action
Training data format:
{
  "state": "...",
  "history": [...],
  "action_taken": "ROLE_ANALYST",
  "outcome_score": 0.87
}
Model types you can use:
Decision Transformer

GPT-style policy model

Multi-agent sequence transformer (best fit for your system)

🚀 FastAPI WebSocket Server
from fastapi import FastAPI, WebSocket
import asyncio
import json
import redis

app = FastAPI()
r = redis.Redis(host="localhost", port=6379, decode_responses=True)

@app.websocket("/ws")
async def websocket_endpoint(ws: WebSocket):
    await ws.accept()

    pubsub = r.pubsub()
    pubsub.subscribe("agent:stream")

    try:
        while True:
            message = pubsub.get_message(ignore_subscribe_messages=True)
            if message:
                await ws.send_text(message["data"])

            await asyncio.sleep(0.01)

    except Exception as e:
        await ws.close()

🧠 Python Implementation
import random
from dataclasses import dataclass

@dataclass
class Agent:
    id: str
    role: str

    def propose(self, task):
        return {
            "agent": self.id,
            "proposal": f"Solution for {task}",
            "confidence": random.random()
        }

    def critique(self, proposal):
        return {
            "agent": self.id,
            "score": random.uniform(0, 1),
            "feedback": "improve reasoning depth"
        }
⚔️ Debate Engine
class DebateEngine:
    def __init__(self, agents):
        self.agents = agents

    def run_debate(self, task):
        proposals = [a.propose(task) for a in self.agents]

        critiques = []
        for p in proposals:
            for a in self.agents:
                critiques.append(a.critique(p))

        scores = {}
        for c in critiques:
            scores[c["agent"]] = scores.get(c["agent"], 0) + c["score"]

        winner = max(scores, key=scores.get)

        return {
            "winner": winner,
            "scores": scores,
            "proposals": proposals
        }
🔥 4. Transformer Policy Training Loop (REPLACES PPO)
This is your learning brain.

🧠 Idea
You train a model to predict:

state → action (role selection)
📦 Dataset format
{
  "state": "user task + context",
  "history": "agent debate logs",
  "action": "ROLE_ANALYST",
  "reward": 0.92
}
🤖 PyTorch Decision Transformer (simplified)
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

class PolicyTransformer(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, vocab_size, d_model=256):
        super().__init__()
        self.embed = nn.Embedding(vocab_size, d_model)
        self.transformer = nn.TransformerEncoder(
            nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(d_model, nhead=8),
            num_layers=4
        )
        self.head = nn.Linear(d_model, vocab_size)

    def forward(self, x):
        x = self.embed(x)
        x = self.transformer(x)
        return self.head(x)
🎯 Training loop
def train(model, data_loader, optimizer):
    loss_fn = nn.CrossEntropyLoss()

    for batch in data_loader:
        states, actions = batch

        preds = model(states)

        loss = loss_fn(preds.view(-1, preds.size(-1)), actions.view(-1))

        optimizer.zero_grad()
        loss.backward()
        optimizer.step()
🔁 Key difference from PPO
PPO	Transformer Policy
reward optimization	sequence learning
unstable	stable scaling
slow	fast iteration
single-agent focus	multi-agent ready

🌐 WebSocket Hook
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

export function useAgentStream() {
  const [events, setEvents] = useState([]);

  useEffect(() => {
    const ws = new WebSocket("ws://localhost:8000/ws");

    ws.onmessage = (msg) => {
      setEvents((prev) => [...prev, JSON.parse(msg.data)]);
    };

    return () => ws.close();
  }, []);

  return events;
}
🧠 Live Event Feed
export default function EventStream() {
  const events = useAgentStream();

  return (
    <div>
      {events.map((e, i) => (
        <div key={i}>
          <b>{e.event_type}</b> — {e.payload.content}
        </div>
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}

🧬 1. Agent Genome Representation
from dataclasses import dataclass
import random
import copy

@dataclass
class AgentGenome:
    id: str
    role: str
    reasoning_style: str
    risk_tolerance: float
    memory_depth: int
    debate_aggressiveness: float
    policy_weights: dict  # abstract representation

    def clone(self):
        return copy.deepcopy(self)
🧪 2. Mutation Engine (Controlled Evolution)
class MutationEngine:

    def mutate(self, agent: AgentGenome):
        new_agent = agent.clone()

        mutation_strength = 0.1

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 106: # mutate continuous traits (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

new_agent.risk_tolerance += random.uniform(-mutation_strength, mutation_strength)
        new_agent.debate_aggressiveness += random.uniform(-mutation_strength, mutation_strength)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 107: # clamp values (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

new_agent.risk_tolerance = max(0, min(1, new_agent.risk_tolerance))
        new_agent.debate_aggressiveness = max(0, min(1, new_agent.debate_aggressiveness))

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 108: # mutate categorical traits (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

if random.random() < 0.2:
            new_agent.reasoning_style = random.choice(
                ["analytical", "creative", "skeptical", "planner"]
            )

        new_agent.id = f"{agent.id}_mut"

        return new_agent
🔀 3. Crossover Engine (Agent Breeding)
class CrossoverEngine:

    def crossover(self, a: AgentGenome, b: AgentGenome):
        child = a.clone()

        child.risk_tolerance = (a.risk_tolerance + b.risk_tolerance) / 2
        child.debate_aggressiveness = (a.debate_aggressiveness + b.debate_aggressiveness) / 2

        child.reasoning_style = random.choice([a.reasoning_style, b.reasoning_style])

        child.id = f"{a.id}_{b.id}_child"

        return child
📊 4. Evaluator Engine (Fitness Function)
This is where intelligence becomes measurable.

class Evaluator:

    def score(self, agent, task_results):
        return (
            task_results["accuracy"] * 0.5 +
            task_results["speed"] * 0.2 +
            task_results["reasoning_depth"] * 0.2 +
            (1 - task_results["conflicts"]) * 0.1
        )
🧪 5. Execution Sandbox (VERY IMPORTANT)
Prevents unsafe uncontrolled behavior.

class Sandbox:

    def run_agent(self, agent, task):

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 109: # simulate execution safely (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

result = {
            "accuracy": random.uniform(0.5, 1),
            "speed": random.uniform(0.3, 1),
            "reasoning_depth": random.uniform(0.4, 1),
            "conflicts": random.uniform(0, 0.5),
        }
        return result
🏆 6. Evolution Controller (THE CORE LOOP)
class EvolutionController:

    def __init__(self):
        self.mutator = MutationEngine()
        self.crossover = CrossoverEngine()
        self.evaluator = Evaluator()
        self.sandbox = Sandbox()

    def evolve(self, population, task):
        scored_population = []

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 110: # 1. evaluate all agents (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

for agent in population:
            result = self.sandbox.run_agent(agent, task)
            score = self.evaluator.score(agent, result)
            scored_population.append((agent, score))

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 111: # 2. sort best agents (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

scored_population.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)

        top_agents = [a for a, _ in scored_population[:5]]

        new_population = []

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 112: # 3. keep elites (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

new_population.extend(top_agents)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 113: # 4. mutate top agents (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

for agent in top_agents:
            new_population.append(self.mutator.mutate(agent))

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 114: # 5. crossover best agents (from *NEW Roles.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

for i in range(len(top_agents)-1):
            child = self.crossover.crossover(top_agents[i], top_agents[i+1])
            new_population.append(child)

        return new_population
🔁 7. Full Self-Evolution Loop
def run_evolution_system(initial_population, tasks):
    controller = EvolutionController()
    population = initial_population

    for generation in range(50):

        task = random.choice(tasks)

        population = controller.evolve(population, task)

        print(f"Generation {generation} complete | population: {len(population)}")

Because over time:

weak reasoning styles disappear

successful debate behaviors dominate

agent personalities drift toward high-performance clusters

system automatically discovers new “roles”

🧬 2. NEURAL GENOME AGENTS (TRANSFORMER-BASED REPLACEMENT)
🔥 Upgrade from “traits” → “weights”

You now use:

🧠 Agent = Small Policy Transformer
class NeuralAgent(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.policy = PolicyTransformer()
        self.memory_router = nn.Linear(256, 256)

    def forward(self, state, memory):
        x = self.policy(state)
        x = self.memory_router(x + memory)
        return x
🧬 Evolution = weight mutation
def mutate_weights(model, sigma=0.01):
    with torch.no_grad():
        for p in model.parameters():
            p.add_(torch.randn_like(p) * sigma)

⚠️ This is “neural evolution”, not parameter tweaking.

🤖 4. REWARD MODEL (REPLACES MANUAL SCORING)
❌ Before:
human-defined score functions
✅ Now:
learned reward model (like RLHF)
🧠 Reward model architecture
class RewardModel(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        self.encoder = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(d_model=256, nhead=8)
        self.head = nn.Linear(256, 1)

    def forward(self, trajectory):
        x = self.encoder(trajectory)
        return torch.sigmoid(self.head(x.mean(dim=1)))
📊 Training data
{
  "trajectory": "agent debate + action log",
  "label": 0.92
}
⚡ Why this matters

Now agents optimize:

“what the system thinks is good behavior”

instead of hard-coded rules.

🔁 5. SELF-WRITING AGENTS (CODE MUTATION LAYER)

This is the most dangerous + powerful part.

🧠 Concept

Agents can:

propose code changes
test them in sandbox
accept only if reward improves
🧩 Safe architecture
Agent → Code Proposal → Sandbox Execution → Reward Check → Accept/Reject
🧪 Code mutation engine
class CodeMutator:

    def propose_patch(self, code):
        return code.replace("risk=0.5", "risk=0.7")

    def validate(self, old_score, new_score):
        return new_score > old_score
🧠 Critical safety rule

Only allow:

AST-based edits (not raw exec edits)
sandbox execution
version rollback

def sync_population(nodes):

🌐 3. Ray Distributed Agent Swarm
import ray

ray.init(address="auto")

@ray.remote
class AgentNode:

    def run(self, agent, task):
        return {
            "agent": agent["id"],
            "result": "done",
            "score": 0.8
        }
🔁 4. Evolution Loop (core brain)
def evolution_step(population, tasks):
    results = []

    for agent in population:
        task = random.choice(tasks)
        result = run_sandbox(agent, task)
        score = reward_model(result)
        results.append((agent, score))

    results.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)

    elites = [a for a, _ in results[:5]]

    new_pop = elites + mutate(elites) + crossover(elites)

    return new_pop

  };

  return () => ws.close();
}, []);

🔥 3. SELF-MODIFYING CODE ENGINE (AST SAFE SYSTEM)
This is the most sensitive system — so we design it like a compiler, not a free-form AI writer.

⚠️ Core rule
Agents NEVER directly execute raw modified code.

They only produce AST diffs.

🧠 AST mutation pipeline
Code → Parse AST → Apply mutation → Validate → Sandbox test → Accept/Reject
🧬 Example AST mutation
Original code:
def decide(risk):
    return risk * 0.5
Mutation proposal:
{
  "type": "modify_constant",
  "target": "0.5",
  "new_value": "0.7"
}
🧠 AST engine (Python)
import ast
import copy

class ASTMutator:

    def mutate_constant(self, tree):
        for node in ast.walk(tree):
            if isinstance(node, ast.Constant) and isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                node.value *= 1.1
        return tree
🧪 Sandbox validation
def validate_change(old_score, new_score):
    return new_score > old_score and new_score < old_score * 1.3
🔁 Rollback system
class VersionControl:

    def __init__(self):
        self.snapshots = []

    def commit(self, code):
        self.snapshots.append(code)

    def rollback(self):
        return self.snapshots[-1]
📜 Audit trail
Every mutation is logged:

{
  "mutation_id": "123",
  "agent": "A9",
  "change": "risk multiplier 0.5 → 0.7",
  "result": "accepted",
  "score_delta": +0.04
}

🧠 Fusion algorithm
def fuse_memory(episode, vector_db):
    summary = summarize(episode)
    embedding = embed(summary)

    vector_db.store({
        "embedding": embedding,
        "summary": summary,
        "episode_ref": episode["id"]
    })
🔥 Retrieval flow
def recall(query):
    sem = vector_db.search(query)
    episodic = load_episode(sem["episode_ref"])

    return merge(sem, episodic)
⚡ Why this matters

Agents now:

remember what happened
understand what it means
generalize across experiences

    score_b = evaluate(species_b, task)

⚔️ Battle system
def compete(species_a, species_b, task):
    score_a = evaluate(species_a, task)
    score_b = evaluate(species_b, task)

    if score_a > score_b:
        species_a.resources += 1
        species_b.resources -= 1
    else:
        species_b.resources += 1
        species_a.resources -= 1
🧠 Ecosystem pressure effects
Pressure type	Effect
resource scarcity	faster evolution
competition	specialization
dominance	species collapse
diversity pressure	mutation increase

“dominant cognition patterns”

🧠 1. SELF-BALANCING ECOSYSTEM (NO COLLAPSE)
🚨 The real problem you’re solving

Without control, agent ecosystems usually:

collapse into one dominant strategy
or explode into random mutation noise
or stagnate (no evolution pressure)

So we add a homeostasis system.

⚖️ Ecosystem balance equation

We maintain 3 global pressures:

Diversity ↑
Performance ↑
Stability ↑

But they compete:

Fitness = performance
Penalty = collapse risk + monoculture
Reward = diversity + novelty
🧠 Stability controller
def ecosystem_health(population):
    diversity = measure_diversity(population)
    variance = measure_behavior_variance(population)
    collapse_risk = 1 - diversity

    return {
        "health": diversity * 0.5 + variance * 0.5,
        "collapse_risk": collapse_risk
    }
🔁 Auto-correction loop
def stabilize(population):
    metrics = ecosystem_health(population)

    if metrics["collapse_risk"] > 0.7:
        inject_random_species(population)

    if metrics["health"] < 0.4:
        increase_mutation_rate(population)

    return population

def detect_specialization(agents):

🧬 3. AUTOMATED SPECIES CREATION RULES

def should_create_new_species(population):

        return True
🔀 Species split rule
def split_species(species):
    if internal_variance(species) > threshold:
        return cluster(species.agents)
🔥 Species merge rule
def merge_species(a, b):
    if similarity(a, b) > 0.9:
        return combine(a, b)
⚰️ Species extinction rule
def extinction(species):
    if species.fitness < 0.2 for 10 cycles:
        remove(species)

temporary structures in evolutionary pressure fields

🧠 3. ECOSYSTEM ENGINE (CORE SIMULATOR)
class Ecosystem:

    def __init__(self):
        self.species = []

    def step(self, task):
        results = []

        for s in self.species:
            results.append(s.evaluate(task))

        self.apply_evolution(results)
        self.balance_system()

    def balance_system(self):
        self.species = stabilize(self.species)
🔁 4. EVOLUTION LOOP
def evolve_system(ecosystem, tasks):

    for t in tasks:
        ecosystem.step(t)

        if should_create_new_species(ecosystem.species):
            ecosystem.species.append(create_species())

🧬 4. EMERGENT LANGUAGE BETWEEN SPECIES (ADVANCED RESEARCH LAYER)
🧠 CORE IDEA

🧬 1. COMMUNICATION MODEL

Each message is NOT text.

It is a latent token packet:

{
  "sender_species": "S1",
  "receiver_species": "S3",
  "message_vector": [0.12, -0.44, 0.88],
  "context": "task-solving state"
}
🧠 2. SYMBOL EMERGENCE MECHANISM

We introduce a compression bottleneck:

agents are rewarded for shorter + more effective communication

🧪 Symbol creation rule
def create_symbol(meaning_vector):
    return hash(quantize(meaning_vector))

🧠 1. LANGUAGE PHYSICS ENGINE
(Simulated laws of meaning attraction/repulsion)
💡 CORE IDEA
Meaning is treated like a physical field, not a static label.

So symbols behave like particles:

attract similar meanings

repel conflicting meanings

drift over time under pressure

🌌 SEMANTIC SPACE MODEL
Symbol = particle in vector space
Meaning = position in semantic field
Usage = force applied to position
⚡ 1. ATTRACTION FORCE
Symbols used in similar contexts move closer:

def attraction(a, b):
    return cosine_similarity(a.vec, b.vec)
⚡ 2. REPULSION FORCE
Conflicting usage pushes symbols apart:

def repulsion(a, b):
    return max(0, 1 - cosine_similarity(a.vec, b.vec))
🧠 3. FIELD UPDATE RULE
def update_symbol(symbol, neighbors):
    force = sum([
        attraction(symbol, n) - repulsion(symbol, n)
        for n in neighbors
    ])

    symbol.vec += force * learning_rate
🔥 RESULT
You get:

stable meaning clusters

drifting dialect zones

emergent semantic gravity wells

def global_attention(events_all_species):
    scored = []

    for e in events_all_species:
        score = (
            e["reward"] * 0.4 +
            e["novelty"] * 0.3 +
            e["cross_species_impact"] * 0.3
        )
        scored.append((e, score))

    return sorted(scored, key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)[:50]

def stabilize(population):

    m = stability_metrics(population)

    if m["collapse_risk"] > 0.7:
        inject_new_species(population)

    if m["diversity"] < 0.3:
        increase_mutation(population)

    if m["fitness_variance"] > 0.9:
        reduce_selection_pressure(population)

    return population

def update_positions(agents):

🌐 1. DECISION INTENSITY FIELD
Every point in the system has a scalar value:

Intensity(x) = amount of decision activity at position x
🧠 FIELD COMPUTATION
def compute_heatmap(agents):

    grid = {}

    for a in agents:
        pos = discretize(a.pos)

        intensity = (
            a.decision_entropy * 0.4 +
            a.reward_signal * 0.4 +
            a.attention_weight * 0.2
        )

        grid[pos] = grid.get(pos, 0) + intensity

    return grid
🔥 VISUAL OUTPUT
You render it as:

red = high decision pressure

yellow = moderate reasoning

blue = low/no activity

🌌 3D RENDERING CONCEPT
<mesh>
  <planeGeometry args={[20, 20, 64, 64]} />
  <shaderMaterial uniforms={{
    heatmap: { value: data }
  }} />
</mesh>

🔁 2. TIME INDEX SYSTEM
class TimeIndex:

    def __init__(self):
        self.snapshots = {}

    def store(self, t, state):
        self.snapshots[t] = deep_copy(state)
⏪ 3. REPLAY ENGINE
def replay(time_index, start, end, speed=1):

    for t in range(start, end):

        state = time_index.snapshots[t]

        render(state)

        sleep(1 / speed)

🧠 1. CAUSAL TRACE ENGINE
(WHY decisions happened, not just when)
💡 CORE IDEA
Every decision in your system must carry:

a causal chain of influences

Not logs. Not traces.

But structured reason graphs.

🌐 1. CAUSAL GRAPH MODEL
Each decision becomes a node in a graph:

Decision D = f(inputs, memory, attention, species pressure)
🧠 CAUSAL NODE STRUCTURE
class CausalNode:
    def __init__(self, decision_id):
        self.inputs = []
        self.memory_refs = []
        self.attention_sources = []
        self.reward_signals = []
        self.parent_decisions = []
🔁 CAUSAL EDGE TYPES
You don’t just store connections — you classify them:

- memory_influence
- attention_bias
- reward_driven_shift
- species_pressure
- mutation_trigger
🧠 TRACE CONSTRUCTION
def build_causal_trace(decision):

    return {
        "decision": decision.id,
        "causes": {
            "memory": decision.memory_refs,
            "attention": decision.attention_sources,
            "reward": decision.reward_signals,
            "population_pressure": decision.species_context
        }
    }
🔥 2. CAUSAL QUERY ENGINE
This is what makes it powerful.

You can ask:

“Why did species S3 collapse?”

🧠 QUERY RESOLUTION
def explain(decision_id):

    trace = load_causal_graph(decision_id)

    return {
        "primary_cause": rank(trace.causes),
        "chain": traverse_backwards(trace),
        "confidence": compute_confidence(trace)
    }

class AnalystAgent:

    def __init__(self):
        self.focus = ["causality", "evolution", "anomalies"]

    def analyze(self, snapshot):
        return self.summarize(
            self.extract_signals(snapshot)
        )
🔍 3. INPUT SIGNALS (WHAT IT READS)
The analyst does NOT read raw agents directly.

It reads structured signals:

def extract_signals(snapshot):

    return {
        "system_health": snapshot.health_metrics,
        "species_changes": snapshot.species_deltas,
        "causal_events": snapshot.causal_graphs,
        "attention_hotspots": snapshot.heatmap,
        "memory_shifts": snapshot.memory_updates
    }
🧠 4. EXPLANATION GENERATION PIPELINE
🧩 STEP 1 — INTERPRETATION
def interpret(signals):
    return {
        "key_event": detect_dominant_event(signals),
        "secondary_effects": find_side_effects(signals),
        "anomalies": detect_anomalies(signals)
    }
🧩 STEP 2 — CAUSAL STORY BUILDING
def build_story(analysis):

    return [
        "what changed",
        "why it changed",
        "what influenced it",
        "what might happen next"
    ]
🧩 STEP 3 — NATURAL LANGUAGE SYNTHESIS
def generate_report(story):

    return f"""
SYSTEM ANALYSIS REPORT:

Primary Event:
{story['key_event']}

Causal Explanation:
{story['causal_chain']}

System Impact:
{story['impact_summary']}

Prediction:
{story['forecast']}
"""

🌐 6. REAL-TIME ANALYSIS LOOP
def analyst_loop(kernel):

    while True:

        snapshot = kernel.get_snapshot()

        signals = extract_signals(snapshot)

        interpretation = interpret(signals)

        report = generate_report(interpretation)

        stream_to_dashboard(report)

🧠 SELF-OBSERVING SYSTEM MODE
(System analyzes and improves its own architecture)
💡 CORE IDEA

Your ecosystem becomes a closed-loop engineering system:

System runs → System logs everything → System analyzes itself → System proposes improvements → Humans (or policies) approve → System updates
🧠 1. META-ARCHITECTURE LAYER

You add a second brain above the system:

        ┌─────────────────────────────┐
        │   SELF-OBSERVATION LAYER    │
        │ (meta-analysis + critique)  │
        └────────────┬────────────────┘
                     ↓
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐

🔁 3. SELF-OBSERVATION LOOP
def self_observe(system_state):

    metrics = collect_metrics(system_state)

    issues = detect_inefficiencies(metrics)

    hypotheses = generate_improvements(issues)

    ranked = rank_by_expected_gain(hypotheses)

    return ranked
🧠 4. SYSTEM CRITIC ENGINE

This is the “inner reviewer”:

class SystemCritic:

    def analyze(self, logs):

        return {
            "bottlenecks": detect_bottlenecks(logs),
            "instability_sources": detect_instability(logs),
            "architecture_flaws": detect_design_issues(logs)
        }
🔍 5. IMPROVEMENT PROPOSAL ENGINE

The system generates structured upgrade suggestions

def propose_changes(issues):

    return [
        {
            "type": "optimization",
            "change": "cache causal traces",
            "expected_gain": 0.23
        },
        {
            "type": "architecture",
            "change": "split species scheduler into parallel shards",
            "expected_gain": 0.41
        }
    ]
⚖️ 6. SAFE EVOLUTION GATE (CRITICAL)

Nothing is auto-applied blindly.

You need a governance layer:

def approve_change(change):

    if change["risk"] > threshold:
        require_human_review()

    if simulation_test(change) < baseline:
        reject(change)

    return approve(change)
🧪 7. “SHADOW SIMULATION MODE”

Before applying changes:

system runs a fake version of itself

Current System → Shadow Clone → Apply Change → Compare Outcomes
🧠 8. META-LEARNING LOOP

System improves how it improves itself:

def update_meta_policy(results):

    meta_model.learn(
        inputs=system_improvements,
        targets=performance_delta
    )

🧱 2. ARCHITECTURE CHANGE AS “PATCH OBJECT”
Every change is structured:

class ArchitecturePatch:
    def __init__(self):
        self.id = None
        self.target_module = None
        self.change_type = None  # optimize / split / merge / replace
        self.diff = None
        self.expected_gain = None
        self.risk_score = None
🔍 3. REFACTOR DETECTION ENGINE
def detect_refactor_opportunities(system_state):

    return [
        {
            "target": "event_bus",

            "suggestion": "introduce sharded stream"
        },
        {
            "target": "memory_layer",

🧪 4. SHADOW REPLICA SIMULATION
This is critical for safety:

Production System
        ↓
Shadow Clone (isolated)
        ↓
Apply Patch
        ↓
Measure performance delta
def simulate_patch(patch, system):

    shadow = clone(system)

    apply(patch, shadow)

    return evaluate(shadow)
⚖️ 5. SAFETY GATE
No direct deployment without checks:

def approve_patch(patch, result):

    if patch.risk_score > 0.6:
        return "REJECT"

    if result.performance_gain < baseline:
        return "REJECT"

    return "APPROVE"
🧬 6. VERSIONED SYSTEM EVOLUTION
Each accepted change becomes a version node

v1.0 → v1.1 → v1.2 → v2.0
         ↑        ↑
      patch A   patch B
🧠 7. ROLLBACK SYSTEM
def rollback(version_id):

    system.load_snapshot(version_id)

    log("rollback executed")

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Dict

@dataclass
class SynopticState:
    domains: Dict[str, float]
    dominant_domain: str
    coherence: float
    conflict: float
✅ B. Aggregation Weights

Map agents → domains:

DOMAIN_MAP = {
    "emotion": ["emotion_agent"],
    "reasoning": ["reasoning_agent", "planner_agent"],

🧠 2. Planetary Mapping Logic

Now convert synoptic → planetary properties

def synoptic_to_planets(state: SynopticState):
    planets = []

    for name, value in state.domains.items():
        planets.append({
            "name": name,
            "size": 1.5 + value * 4.0,
            "orbit_radius": 5 + hash(name) % 5,
            "orbit_speed": 0.2 + value * 1.5,
            "glow": value,
            "color": get_color(name, value),
        })

    return planets
🎨 Color Logic
def get_color(name, value):
    if name == "emotion":
        return "#ff4d6d" if value > 0.5 else "#6c8cff"
    if name == "reasoning":
        return "#4da6ff"
    if name == "memory":
        return "#9b59b6"
    if name == "risk":
        return "#ff3b3b" if value > 0.4 else "#ffaa00"
    if name == "personality":
        return "#00d4ff"
    return "#ffffff"

⚛️ 4. Frontend — Temporal Smoothing

Even with backend smoothing, frontend must interpolate.

✅ A. LERP utility
function lerp(a, b, t) {
  return a + (b - a) * t;
}
✅ B. Smooth Planet State
import { useRef } from "react";
import { useFrame } from "@react-three/fiber";

export default function Planet({ data }) {
  const ref = useRef();

  const state = useRef({
    angle: 0,
    radius: data.orbit_radius,
    size: data.size,
    glow: data.glow
  });

  useFrame((_, delta) => {
    // Smooth transitions
    state.current.radius = lerp(
      state.current.radius,
      data.orbit_radius,
      delta * 2
    );

    state.current.size = lerp(
      state.current.size,
      data.size,
      delta * 2
    );

    state.current.glow = lerp(
      state.current.glow,
      data.glow,
      delta * 3
    );

    // Orbit progression
    state.current.angle += data.orbit_speed * delta;

    ref.current.position.x =
      Math.cos(state.current.angle) * state.current.radius;

    ref.current.position.z =
      Math.sin(state.current.angle) * state.current.radius;

    ref.current.scale.setScalar(state.current.size);
  });

  return (
    <mesh ref={ref}>
      <sphereGeometry args={[1, 32, 32]} />
      <meshStandardMaterial
        emissive={data.color}
        emissiveIntensity={state.current.glow * 2}
        color={data.color}
      />
    </mesh>
  );
}
🧠 5. Predictive Orbit Behavior (Frontend)

Now the important part: motion influenced by velocity

✅ A. Add Orbit Drift
state.current.angle +=
  (data.orbit_speed + data.velocity * 2) * delta;
✅ B. Add Radial Breathing
const pulse =
  Math.sin(state.current.angle * 2) * data.velocity * 2;

const dynamicRadius =
  state.current.radius + pulse;

ref.current.position.x =
  Math.cos(state.current.angle) * dynamicRadius;

ref.current.position.z =
  Math.sin(state.current.angle) * dynamicRadius;
✅ C. Add Anticipation (THIS is key)

Instead of jumping radius → ease toward target_radius

state.current.radius = lerp(
  state.current.radius,
  data.target_radius,
  delta * (1 + data.velocity * 3)
);
🔥 6. Visual Intelligence Effects

These are small—but make a huge difference.

✨ A. Conflict Distortion
if (globalConflict > 0.6) {
  ref.current.position.y =
    Math.sin(state.current.angle * 10) * 0.5;
}
✨ B. Dominant Planet Highlight
const intensityBoost =
  data.isDominant ? 3.5 : 2;

emissiveIntensity={state.current.glow * intensityBoost}
⚠️ Reality Check (important)

If you skip these rules, this system will look like garbage:

❌ Don’t:
Use raw backend values directly
Update position instantly
Ignore velocity
✅ Do:
Smooth twice (backend + frontend)
Predict slightly (200–400ms)
Keep motion consistent
🚀 What you just unlocked

Now your system has:

Inertia → feels physical
Prediction → feels intelligent
Continuity → feels alive

This is the difference between:

“cool visualization”
vs
“this thing looks like it’s thinking”

✅ A. Planet Component
// src/components/Planet.jsx
import { useRef } from "react";
import { useFrame } from "@react-three/fiber";

export default function Planet({ data }) {
  const ref = useRef();
  const t = useRef(0);

  useFrame((_, delta) => {
    t.current += delta * data.orbit_speed;

    ref.current.position.x =
      Math.cos(t.current) * data.orbit_radius;

    ref.current.position.z =
      Math.sin(t.current) * data.orbit_radius;
  });

  return (
    <mesh ref={ref}>
      <sphereGeometry args={[data.size, 32, 32]} />
      <meshStandardMaterial
        emissive={data.color}
        emissiveIntensity={data.glow * 2}
        color={data.color}
      />
    </mesh>
  );
}
✅ B. Core (Identity Kernel Visual)
function Core() {
  return (
    <mesh>
      <sphereGeometry args={[2, 32, 32]} />
      <meshStandardMaterial
        emissive="#ffffff"
        emissiveIntensity={2}
      />
    </mesh>
  );
}

      <pointLight position={[0, 0, 0]} intensity={2} />

      <Core />

      {planets.map((p, i) => (
        <Planet key={i} data={p} />
      ))}
    </>
  );
}

// src/components/Planet.jsx
import { useRef } from "react";
import { useFrame } from "@react-three/fiber";

export default function Planet({ data }) {
  const ref = useRef();
  const t = useRef(0);

  useFrame((_, delta) => {
    t.current += delta * data.orbit_speed;

    ref.current.position.x =
      Math.cos(t.current) * data.orbit_radius;

    ref.current.position.z =
      Math.sin(t.current) * data.orbit_radius;
  });

  return (
    <mesh ref={ref}>
      <sphereGeometry args={[data.size, 32, 32]} />
      <meshStandardMaterial
        emissive={data.color}
        emissiveIntensity={data.glow * 2}
        color={data.color}
      />
    </mesh>
  );
}

✅ B. Core (Identity Kernel Visual)
function Core() {
  return (
    <mesh>
      <sphereGeometry args={[2, 32, 32]} />
      <meshStandardMaterial
        emissive="#ffffff"
        emissiveIntensity={2}
      />
    </mesh>
  );
}

// src/components/PlanetarySystem.jsx
import Planet from "./Planet";

export default function PlanetarySystem({ planets }) {
  return (
    <>
      <ambientLight intensity={0.4} />
      <pointLight position={[0, 0, 0]} intensity={2} />

      <Core />

      {planets.map((p, i) => (
        <Planet key={i} data={p} />
      ))}
    </>
  );
}

import { useFrame } from "@react-three/fiber";

✅ A. Add Stateful Smoother

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 166: # server/systems/synoptic_smoother.py (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class SynopticSmoother:
    def __init__(self, alpha=0.2):
        self.alpha = alpha
        self.prev = {}

    def smooth(self, domains: dict):
        smoothed = {}

        for k, v in domains.items():
            prev_v = self.prev.get(k, v)
            new_v = (1 - self.alpha) * prev_v + self.alpha * v
            smoothed[k] = new_v

        self.prev = smoothed
        return smoothed
✅ B. Add Velocity Tracking (for prediction)
class SynopticDynamics:
    def __init__(self):
        self.prev = {}
        self.velocity = {}

    def update(self, current: dict):
        vel = {}

        for k, v in current.items():
            prev_v = self.prev.get(k, v)
            vel[k] = v - prev_v

        self.velocity = vel
        self.prev = current

        return vel
✅ C. Integrate into pipeline

INFLUENCE_MATRIX = {
    "emotion": {
        "reasoning": -0.3,   # strong emotion reduces reasoning clarity
        "memory": 0.2,
        "risk": 0.4,
        "personality": 0.3
    },
    "reasoning": {
        "emotion": -0.2,
        "risk": 0.3,
        "memory": 0.4
    },
    "memory": {
        "emotion": 0.3,
        "reasoning": 0.2
    },
    "risk": {
        "emotion": 0.5,
        "reasoning": -0.3
    },
    "personality": {
        "emotion": 0.4,
        "reasoning": 0.2
    }
}
🧠 B. Apply Influence
def apply_cognitive_influence(domains: dict):
    updated = domains.copy()

    for source, targets in INFLUENCE_MATRIX.items():
        source_val = domains.get(source, 0.0)

        for target, weight in targets.items():
            influence = source_val * weight
            updated[target] += influence

🌌 3. Gravitational Strength Model

✅ A. Mass Formula
mass = activation^2
👉 Why?

✅ B. Backend Planet Upgrade
def synoptic_to_planets(domains, velocity):
    planets = []

    for name, value in domains.items():
        mass = value ** 2

        planets.append({
            "name": name,
            "mass": mass,
            "size": 1.5 + value * 4,
            "orbit_radius": 6 + hash(name) % 4,
            "orbit_speed": 0.3 + value,
            "glow": value,
            "velocity": velocity.get(name, 0.0),
            "color": get_color(name, value)
        })

    return planets
⚛️ 4. Frontend — Gravitational Interaction
Now we simulate interaction between planets.

✅ A. Add Velocity Vector (not just angle)
Update your planet state:

const state = useRef({
  position: [0, 0, 0],
  velocity: [0, 0, 0]
});
✅ B. Compute Influence Per Frame
function computeForces(planets) {
  const forces = planets.map(() => [0, 0, 0]);

  for (let i = 0; i < planets.length; i++) {
    for (let j = 0; j < planets.length; j++) {
      if (i === j) continue;

      const p1 = planets[i];
      const p2 = planets[j];

      const dx = p2.pos[0] - p1.pos[0];
      const dz = p2.pos[2] - p1.pos[2];

      const dist = Math.sqrt(dx * dx + dz * dz) + 0.01;

      // "Cognitive gravity"
      const force =
        (p1.mass * p2.mass) / (dist * dist);

      forces[i][0] += (dx / dist) * force;
      forces[i][2] += (dz / dist) * force;
    }
  }

  return forces;
}
🧠 C. Apply Forces (smoothed, not chaotic)
useFrame((_, delta) => {
  const forces = computeForces(allPlanets);

  forces.forEach((f, i) => {
    const p = planetStates[i];

    // Apply damping (VERY IMPORTANT)
    p.velocity[0] += f[0] * delta;
    p.velocity[2] += f[2] * delta;

    p.velocity[0] *= 0.92;
    p.velocity[2] *= 0.92;

    p.position[0] += p.velocity[0];
    p.position[2] += p.velocity[2];
  });
});
🔮 5. Cognitive Constraints (prevents chaos)

const dist = Math.sqrt(
  x * x + z * z
);

const correction = (targetRadius - dist) * 0.1;

p.velocity[0] += (x / dist) * correction;
p.velocity[2] += (z / dist) * correction;
✅ B. Dominant Gravity Bias
if (data.isDominant) {
  p.velocity[0] *= 0.98;
  p.velocity[2] *= 0.98;
}

function computeForces(planets) {
  const forces = planets.map(() => [0, 0, 0]);

  for (let i = 0; i < planets.length; i++) {
    for (let j = 0; j < planets.length; j++) {
      if (i === j) continue;

      const p1 = planets[i];
      const p2 = planets[j];

      const dx = p2.pos[0] - p1.pos[0];
      const dz = p2.pos[2] - p1.pos[2];

      const dist = Math.sqrt(dx * dx + dz * dz) + 0.01;

      // "Cognitive gravity"
      const force =
        (p1.mass * p2.mass) / (dist * dist);

      forces[i][0] += (dx / dist) * force;
      forces[i][2] += (dz / dist) * force;
    }
  }

  return forces;
}

✅ A. Define Event Structure

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 173: # server/systems/narrative_engine.py (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

from dataclasses import dataclass
import time

@dataclass
class NarrativeEvent:
    id: str
    timestamp: float
    intensity: float          # 0–1
    valence: float            # -1 to +1
    event_type: str           # "question", "emotion", "conflict", etc.
    domain_impact: dict       # per-domain weights
✅ B. Event Creation (from user input)

Hook this into your pipeline after perception:

def create_event(text, emotion_valence, contradiction_level):
    intensity = min(1.0, abs(emotion_valence) + contradiction_level)

    event_type = "neutral"

    if contradiction_level > 0.6:
        event_type = "conflict"
    elif abs(emotion_valence) > 0.5:
        event_type = "emotional"
    elif "?" in text:
        event_type = "question"

    domain_impact = {
        "emotion": abs(emotion_valence),
        "reasoning": 0.5 if event_type == "question" else 0.2,
        "risk": contradiction_level,
        "memory": 0.3
    }

    return NarrativeEvent(
        id=str(time.time()),
        timestamp=time.time(),
        intensity=intensity,
        valence=emotion_valence,
        event_type=event_type,
        domain_impact=domain_impact
    )
🌊 3. Shock Wave System

Now the important part.

✅ A. Shock Wave Model

Each event generates a wave:

shock(t) = intensity * e^(-decay * t)
✅ B. Event Manager
import math
import time

class NarrativeEngine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.events = []

    def add_event(self, event):
        self.events.append(event)

    def compute_shock(self):
        now = time.time()
        domain_effects = {}

        for event in self.events:
            dt = now - event.timestamp

            decay = 1.5  # controls how fast it fades
            shock = event.intensity * math.exp(-decay * dt)

            for domain, weight in event.domain_impact.items():
                domain_effects[domain] = domain_effects.get(domain, 0) + shock * weight

        return domain_effects
🧠 4. Integrate with Cognitive System
✅ Combine with existing domains
shock_effects = narrative_engine.compute_shock()

combined = {}

for k in domains:
    combined[k] = domains[k] + shock_effects.get(k, 0.0)

import math
import time

class NarrativeEngine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.events = []

    def add_event(self, event):
        self.events.append(event)

    def compute_shock(self):
        now = time.time()
        domain_effects = {}

        for event in self.events:
            dt = now - event.timestamp

            decay = 1.5  # controls how fast it fades
            shock = event.intensity * math.exp(-decay * dt)

            for domain, weight in event.domain_impact.items():
                domain_effects[domain] = domain_effects.get(domain, 0) + shock * weight

        return domain_effects

⚛️ 6. Frontend — Shock Wave Visualization
✅ A. Expanding Ring Effect
function ShockWave({ position, strength }) {
  const ref = useRef();
  const t = useRef(0);

  useFrame((_, delta) => {
    t.current += delta;

    const scale = 1 + t.current * 5;
    const opacity = Math.exp(-t.current * 2);

    ref.current.scale.set(scale, 1, scale);
    ref.current.material.opacity = opacity;
  });

  return (
    <mesh ref={ref} position={position}>
      <ringGeometry args={[1, 1.2, 32]} />
      <meshBasicMaterial
        transparent
        opacity={1}
        color="#ffffff"
      />
    </mesh>
  );
}
✅ B. Trigger on new event
const [waves, setWaves] = useState([]);

useEffect(() => {
  if (newEvent) {
    setWaves(prev => [
      ...prev,
      {
        id: Date.now(),
        strength: newEvent.intensity
      }
    ]);
  }
}, [newEvent]);

✅ A. Pulse Effect
const shockBoost = data.shock || 0;

state.current.size = lerp(
  state.current.size,
  data.size + shockBoost * 2,
  delta * 4
);
✅ B. Orbit Disturbance
state.current.angle +=
  (data.orbit_speed + shockBoost * 3) * delta;
✅ C. Temporary Instability
if (data.shock > 0.4) {
  ref.current.position.y =
    Math.sin(time * 10) * data.shock;
}

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
import time

@dataclass
class NarrativeArc:
    id: str
    arc_type: str              # "conflict", "trust_building", "curiosity", etc.
    strength: float           # 0–1
    trend: float              # increasing or decreasing
    last_updated: float
    event_count: int = 0
✅ B. Arc Engine
class NarrativeArcEngine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.arcs = {}

    def update(self, event):
        arc_type = self.map_event_to_arc(event)

        arc = self.arcs.get(arc_type)

        if not arc:
            arc = NarrativeArc(
                id=arc_type,
                arc_type=arc_type,
                strength=0.1,
                trend=0.0,
                last_updated=time.time(),
                event_count=0
            )

@dataclass
class NarrativeArc:
    id: str
    arc_type: str              # "conflict", "trust_building", "curiosity", etc.
    strength: float           # 0–1
    trend: float              # increasing or decreasing
    last_updated: float
    event_count: int = 0
✅ B. Arc Engine
class NarrativeArcEngine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.arcs = {}

    def update(self, event):
        arc_type = self.map_event_to_arc(event)

        arc = self.arcs.get(arc_type)

        if not arc:
            arc = NarrativeArc(
                id=arc_type,
                arc_type=arc_type,
                strength=0.1,
                trend=0.0,
                last_updated=time.time(),
                event_count=0
            )

        arc.event_count += 1
        arc.last_updated = time.time()

        self.arcs[arc_type] = arc

    def map_event_to_arc(self, event):
        if event.event_type == "conflict":
            return "conflict_arc"
        if event.event_type == "emotional":
            return "emotional_arc"
        if event.event_type == "question":
            return "curiosity_arc"
        return "neutral_arc"
🧹 C. Arc Decay (VERY IMPORTANT)
def decay_arcs(self):
    now = time.time()

    for arc in self.arcs.values():
        dt = now - arc.last_updated

        decay_rate = 0.05
        arc.strength *= (1 - decay_rate * dt)

        if arc.strength < 0.05:
            arc.strength = 0.0

👉 Prevents permanent bias

🧬 3. Personality Drift System
✅ A. Personality Model
personality = {
    "empathy": 0.6,
    "logic": 0.7,
    "caution": 0.5,
    "curiosity": 0.6
}
✅ B. Drift Rules

Map arcs → personality influence:

DRIFT_MATRIX = {
    "conflict_arc": {
        "caution": +0.2,
        "empathy": -0.1
    },
    "emotional_arc": {
        "empathy": +0.2
    },
    "curiosity_arc": {
        "curiosity": +0.3,
        "logic": +0.1
    }
}
✅ C. Apply Drift (slow + bounded)
def apply_personality_drift(personality, arcs):
    new_personality = personality.copy()

    for arc_name, arc in arcs.items():
        influence = DRIFT_MATRIX.get(arc_name, {})

        for trait, weight in influence.items():
            drift = arc.strength * weight * 0.01  # VERY SMALL

            new_personality[trait] += drift


        arc.trend = (arc.trend * 0.8) + (event.intensity * 0.2)

        arc.event_count += 1
        arc.last_updated = time.time()

        self.arcs[arc_type] = arc

    def map_event_to_arc(self, event):
        if event.event_type == "conflict":
            return "conflict_arc"
        if event.event_type == "emotional":
            return "emotional_arc"
        if event.event_type == "question":
            return "curiosity_arc"
        return "neutral_arc"
🧹 C. Arc Decay (VERY IMPORTANT)
def decay_arcs(self):

    for arc in self.arcs.values():

        decay_rate = 0.05
        arc.strength *= (1 - decay_rate * dt)

        if arc.strength < 0.05:
            arc.strength = 0.0

def stabilize_personality(current, base):
    stabilized = {}

    for k in current:
        stabilized[k] = (
            current[k] * 0.9 + base[k] * 0.1
        )

    return stabilized

“add self-reflection + meta-cognition layer

🧠 B. Compute Reflection Metrics

def compute_reflection(synoptic, arcs, personality):

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 185: # 1. Coherence (already exists) (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)

drift = compute_drift(personality)

    return ReflectionState(
        coherence=coherence,
        stability=stability,
        alignment=alignment,
        drift=drift,
        last_updated=time.time()
    )
✅ C. Alignment Function
def compute_alignment(arcs, personality):
    score = 0.0
    total = 0.0

    for arc_name, arc in arcs.items():
        if arc_name == "conflict_arc":
            score += (1 - personality["empathy"]) * arc.strength
            total += arc.strength

        if arc_name == "curiosity_arc":
            score += personality["curiosity"] * arc.strength
            total += arc.strength

    if total == 0:
        return 1.0

    return score / total
✅ D. Drift Function
def compute_drift(personality):
    drift = 0.0

    for k in personality:
        drift += abs(personality[k] - BASE_PERSONALITY[k])

    return drift / len(personality)

    def __init__(self):
        self.last_adjustment = 0

    def decide(self, reflection: ReflectionState):
        actions = []

if time.time() - self.last_adjustment < 2:
            return actions

if reflection.alignment < 0.5:
            actions.append(("realign_personality", 0.03))

        self.last_adjustment = time.time()
        return actions
🛠️ 4. Apply Adjustments
✅ A. Stabilization
def stabilize_system(domains):
    return {
        k: v * 0.95 + (1 / len(domains)) * 0.05
        for k, v in domains.items()
    }
✅ B. Reduce Drift
def reduce_drift(personality):
    for k in personality:
        personality[k] = (
            personality[k] * 0.9 +
            BASE_PERSONALITY[k] * 0.1
        )
    return personality
✅ C. Realign Personality
def realign_personality(personality, arcs):
    for arc_name, arc in arcs.items():
        if arc_name == "curiosity_arc":
            personality["curiosity"] += arc.strength * 0.01

        if arc_name == "conflict_arc":
            personality["empathy"] -= arc.strength * 0.01

    return personality

from dataclasses import dataclass
import time

@dataclass
class Goal:
    id: str
    goal_type: str           # "increase_trust", "reduce_conflict", etc.
    priority: float          # 0–1
    progress: float          # 0–1
    created_at: float
    target: dict             # desired state
🧠 B. Goal Types (keep it small)
GOAL_TEMPLATES = {
    "stabilize": {
        "target": {"conflict": 0.2}
    },
    "build_trust": {
        "target": {"trust": 0.8}
    },
    "increase_curiosity": {
        "target": {"curiosity": 0.75}
    },
    "reduce_risk": {
        "target": {"risk": 0.2}
    }
}

👉 Don’t add 20 goals. 4–6 max.

✅ C. Goal Generator

Goals should come from reflection + arcs

import time

def generate_goals(reflection, arcs):
    goals = []

    if reflection.stability < 0.4:
        goals.append(create_goal("stabilize", 0.9))

    if reflection.alignment < 0.5:
        goals.append(create_goal("build_trust", 0.7))

    if arcs.get("curiosity_arc", None) and arcs["curiosity_arc"].strength > 0.5:
        goals.append(create_goal("increase_curiosity", 0.6))

    if arcs.get("conflict_arc", None) and arcs["conflict_arc"].strength > 0.5:
        goals.append(create_goal("reduce_risk", 0.8))

    return goals


def create_goal(goal_type, priority):
    template = GOAL_TEMPLATES[goal_type]

    return Goal(
        id=f"{goal_type}_{time.time()}",
        goal_type=goal_type,
        priority=priority,
        progress=0.0,
        created_at=time.time(),
        target=template["target"]
    )
📈 3. Goal Evaluation (Progress Tracking)
✅ Measure progress
def evaluate_goal(goal, synoptic, personality):
    progress = 0.0

    for k, target_val in goal.target.items():
        current = synoptic.get(k, personality.get(k, 0.5))

        diff = abs(target_val - current)
        progress += (1 - diff)

    goal.progress = progress / len(goal.target)
    return goal
🧭 4. Planning Layer

class Plan:
    goal_id: str
    actions: list
✅ B. Action Types

ACTIONS = [
    "increase_empathy",
    "increase_reasoning",
    "reduce_volatility",
    "increase_curiosity",
    "decrease_risk"
]
🧠 C. Planner Logic
def create_plan(goal):
    actions = []

    if goal.goal_type == "stabilize":
        actions += ["reduce_volatility", "increase_reasoning"]

    elif goal.goal_type == "build_trust":
        actions += ["increase_empathy"]

    elif goal.goal_type == "increase_curiosity":
        actions += ["increase_curiosity", "increase_reasoning"]

    elif goal.goal_type == "reduce_risk":
        actions += ["decrease_risk", "increase_reasoning"]

    return Plan(goal_id=goal.id, actions=actions)
⚙️ 5. Action Execution Layer
✅ Apply actions to system
def apply_actions(domains, personality, actions):
    for action in actions:

        if action == "increase_empathy":
            personality["empathy"] += 0.02

        elif action == "increase_reasoning":
            domains["reasoning"] += 0.03

        elif action == "reduce_volatility":
            domains = {
                k: v * 0.95 for k, v in domains.items()
            }

        elif action == "increase_curiosity":
            personality["curiosity"] += 0.02

        elif action == "decrease_risk":
            domains["risk"] *= 0.9

    return domains, personality

👉 Keep adjustments small (critical)

⚖️ 3. Multi-Goal Arbitration Engine
🧠 A. Scoring Function (this is critical)
def score_goal(goal, reflection, arcs):
    urgency = 1 - reflection.stability if goal.goal_type == "stabilize" else 0.5

    relevance = 0.0
    if goal.goal_type == "reduce_risk":
        relevance = arcs.get("conflict_arc", {}).strength if "conflict_arc" in arcs else 0

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 203: # priority already exists (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

score = (
        goal.priority * 0.5 +
        urgency * 0.3 +
        relevance * 0.2
    )

    return score
✅ B. Arbitration Logic
def select_active_goals(goals, reflection, arcs, max_active=2):
    scored = []

    for g in goals:
        s = score_goal(g, reflection, arcs)
        scored.append((g, s))

    scored.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)

    return [g for g, _ in scored[:max_active]]

👉 Limit active goals (VERY IMPORTANT)

🧭 4. Hierarchical Planning
✅ A. Decompose Goals
def decompose_goal(goal):
    subgoals = []

    if goal.goal_type == "maintain_stability":
        subgoals.append(create_subgoal("reduce_volatility", goal.id))
        subgoals.append(create_subgoal("increase_reasoning", goal.id))

    if goal.goal_type == "build_relationship":
        subgoals.append(create_subgoal("increase_empathy", goal.id))

    return subgoals


def create_subgoal(goal_type, parent_id):
    return Goal(
        id=f"{goal_type}_{time.time()}",
        goal_type=goal_type,
        level=2,
        priority=0.6,
        progress=0.0,
        parent_id=parent_id,
        created_at=time.time(),
        target={}
    )
✅ B. Convert to Actions (L1)
def subgoal_to_actions(subgoal):
    if subgoal.goal_type == "reduce_volatility":
        return ["reduce_volatility"]

    if subgoal.goal_type == "increase_empathy":
        return ["increase_empathy"]

    if subgoal.goal_type == "increase_reasoning":
        return ["increase_reasoning"]

    return []
🔁 5. Long-Horizon Planning (bounded)
🧠 A. Planning Horizon
MAX_DEPTH = 2   # don’t go deeper
MAX_ACTIONS = 3 # limit execution
✅ B. Build Plan Tree
def build_plan(goals):
    actions = []

    for g in goals:
        subgoals = decompose_goal(g)

        for sg in subgoals[:MAX_DEPTH]:
            actions += subgoal_to_actions(sg)

    return actions[:MAX_ACTIONS]
⚙️ 6. Conflict Resolution Between Goals
✅ A. Detect conflicting actions
CONFLICTS = {
    ("increase_empathy", "reduce_risk"),
}
✅ B. Resolve
def resolve_conflicts(actions):
    final = []

    for a in actions:
        if any((a, b) in CONFLICTS or (b, a) in CONFLICTS for b in final):
            continue
        final.append(a)

    return final

def compute_synoptic(
    swarm_activations,
    smoother,
    dynamics,
    narrative_engine,
    arcs
):

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 209: # 1. base aggregation (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

base = aggregate_synoptic(swarm_activations)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 210: # 2. smoothing (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 211: # 3. velocity (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 212: # 4. narrative shock (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

shock = narrative_engine.compute_shock()

    combined = {
        k: smoothed.get(k, 0) + shock.get(k, 0)
        for k in smoothed
    }

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 213: # 5. normalize (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)

total = sum(combined.values()) + 1e-6
    normalized = {k: v / total for k, v in combined.items()}

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 215: # 7. metrics (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

dominant = max(influenced, key=influenced.get)
    coherence = max(influenced.values())

    vals = sorted(influenced.values(), reverse=True)
    conflict = vals[1] / (vals[0] + 1e-6) if len(vals) > 1 else 0

    return {
        "domains": influenced,
        "dominant": dominant,
        "coherence": coherence,
        "conflict": conflict,
        "trend": velocity
    }

✅ React Component
// SynopticRadial.jsx

export default function SynopticRadial({ domains }) {
  const keys = Object.keys(domains);
  const angleStep = (Math.PI * 2) / keys.length;

  return (
    <svg width={300} height={300}>
      {keys.map((k, i) => {
        const angle = i * angleStep;
        const r = domains[k] * 100;

        const x = 150 + Math.cos(angle) * r;
        const y = 150 + Math.sin(angle) * r;

        return (
          <line
            key={k}
            x1={150}
            y1={150}
            x2={x}
            y2={y}
            stroke="cyan"
          />
        );
      })}
    </svg>
  );
}

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 216: 1. What you’re adding (conceptually) (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

You’re extending Synoptics from:

“predicting what will happen + flagging what should NOT be happening”

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 232: 2. Node.js WebSocket Server (Gateway) (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

server.js
import express from "express";
import http from "http";
import { WebSocketServer } from "ws";
import Redis from "ioredis";

const app = express();
const server = http.createServer(app);
const wss = new WebSocketServer({ server });

const redisSub = new Redis();
const redisPub = new Redis();

const CHANNEL = "synoptics_stream";

// Subscribe to Python engine updates
redisSub.subscribe(CHANNEL);

redisSub.on("message", (channel, message) => {
  if (channel === CHANNEL) {
    broadcast(JSON.parse(message));
  }
});

// WebSocket clients
const clients = new Set();

wss.on("connection", (ws) => {
  clients.add(ws);

  ws.on("message", (msg) => {
    const data = JSON.parse(msg);

    // optional: forward commands to Python engine
    redisPub.publish("synoptics_commands", JSON.stringify(data));
  });

  ws.on("close", () => {
    clients.delete(ws);
  });
});

function broadcast(data) {
  const payload = JSON.stringify(data);
  for (const client of clients) {
    if (client.readyState === 1) {
      client.send(payload);
    }
  }
}

server.listen(8080, () => {
  console.log("Synoptics Gateway running on :8080");
});

engine.py
import asyncio
import json
import redis
import numpy as np
from collections import deque

r = redis.Redis()

state_buffer = deque(maxlen=50)

CHANNEL_OUT = "synoptics_stream"
CHANNEL_IN = "synoptics_commands"

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 234: # ---------- FEATURE ENGINE ---------- (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def extract_features(state):
    return {
        "avg_velocity": np.mean([a["v"] for a in state]),
        "density": len(state),
        "entropy": np.std([a["x"] for a in state])
    }

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 235: # ---------- PREDICTION ---------- (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def predict_next(state):
    return [
        {
            "id": a["id"],
            "x": a["x"] + a["vx"] * 0.8,
            "y": a["y"] + a["vy"] * 0.8,
            "confidence": 0.82
        }
        for a in state
    ]

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 236: # ---------- ANOMALY DETECTION ---------- (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def detect_anomalies(state):
    anomalies = []

    velocities = [a["vx"] for a in state]
    if np.std(velocities) > 2.5:
        anomalies.append({
            "type": "velocity_spike",
            "severity": float(np.std(velocities))
        })

    if len(state) > 0 and np.mean([a["x"] for a in state]) > 100:
        anomalies.append({
            "type": "spatial_drift",
            "severity": 0.7
        })

    return anomalies

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 237: # ---------- MAIN LOOP ---------- (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def run_engine():
    while True:
        raw = r.get("current_state")
        if not raw:
            continue

        state = json.loads(raw)
        state_buffer.append(state)

        prediction = predict_next(state)
        anomalies = detect_anomalies(state)

        features = extract_features(state)

        payload = {
            "type": "synoptics_update",
            "prediction": prediction,
            "anomalies": anomalies,
            "features": features
        }

        r.publish(CHANNEL_OUT, json.dumps(payload))

run_engine()

  Section 239: 5. React Dashboard (Predictive + Anomaly Overlay) (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)

SynopticsDashboard.jsx
import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";

export default function SynopticsDashboard() {
  const canvasRef = useRef(null);
  const [data, setData] = useState(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    const ws = new WebSocket("ws://localhost:8080");

    ws.onmessage = (msg) => {
      setData(JSON.parse(msg.data));
    };

    return () => ws.close();
  }, []);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!data) return;

    const canvas = canvasRef.current;
    const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");

    ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // REAL STATE
    data.prediction?.forEach((a) => {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(a.x, a.y, 4, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(0,255,0,0.8)";
      ctx.fill();
    });

    // ANOMALIES
    data.anomalies?.forEach((a) => {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(200, 200, 30, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.strokeStyle = "red";
      ctx.stroke();
    });

    // PREDICTIVE TRAILS (ghost layer)
    data.prediction?.forEach((a) => {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(a.x + 10, a.y + 10, 3, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(0,150,255,0.3)";
      ctx.fill();
    });
  }, [data]);

  return (
    <div className="w-full h-screen bg-black">
      <canvas ref={canvasRef} width={1200} height={800} />

      <div className="absolute top-4 left-4 text-white">
        <div>Density: {data?.features?.density}</div>
        <div>Entropy: {data?.features?.entropy}</div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

This replaces your current heuristic predictor with a temporal graph model.

What it does

PyTorch Geometric model
gnn_predictor.py
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from torch_geometric.nn import SAGEConv

class SwarmGNN(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, in_dim=4, hidden=64, out_dim=2):
        super().__init__()

        self.conv1 = SAGEConv(in_dim, hidden)
        self.conv2 = SAGEConv(hidden, hidden)

        self.rnn = nn.GRU(hidden, hidden, batch_first=True)
        self.out = nn.Linear(hidden, out_dim)

    def forward(self, x, edge_index, seq):

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 242: # x: node features (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

x = torch.relu(self.conv1(x, edge_index))
        x = torch.relu(self.conv2(x, edge_index))

        x, _ = self.rnn(x.unsqueeze(0))
        x = x.squeeze(0)

        return self.out(x)
Output:
next position (x, y)
learned interaction dynamics
Why this matters

Now your swarm:

learns patterns of movement
adapts over time
predicts emergent structures (not just motion)

  Section 243: 2. Multi-Agent Intent Detection Layer (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is where your system becomes cognitive, not just geometric.

Goal

Infer:

“what each agent is trying to do”

Approach

Use embedding + clustering + temporal shift detection.

Implementation
intent_layer.py
import numpy as np
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans

class IntentDetector:
    def __init__(self, k=5):
        self.kmeans = KMeans(n_clusters=k)

    def build_features(self, agents):
        return np.array([
            [a["vx"], a["vy"], a["x"], a["y"]]
            for a in agents
        ])

    def detect(self, agents):
        X = self.build_features(agents)

        labels = self.kmeans.fit_predict(X)

        intents = {}
        for i, label in enumerate(labels):
            intents.setdefault(label, []).append(agents[i])

        return intents
What you get
“cluster A is converging”
“cluster B is escaping”
“cluster C is unstable”

This feeds directly into anomaly + prediction layers.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 244: 3. Shockwave Propagation System (EVENT CASCADES) (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is your causal engine.

Concept

When something changes:

it propagates influence like physics waves

Model

Graph diffusion process:

impact(t+1)=decay×adjacency×impact(t)
Implementation
shockwave.py
import numpy as np

class ShockwaveEngine:
    def __init__(self, decay=0.85):
        self.decay = decay

    def propagate(self, adjacency, initial_impact, steps=5):
        impact = initial_impact

        history = [impact]

        for _ in range(steps):
            impact = self.decay * adjacency @ impact
            history.append(impact)

        return history
Result

You can now show:

ripple effects from anomalies
cascading failures
“event gravity”

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 245: 4. Self-Correcting Swarm Stabilization (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is your autonomous control system.

Goal

If swarm becomes unstable:

reduce entropy
rebalance clusters
dampen oscillations
Control rule
adjustment=−λ×gradient(entropy)
Implementation
stabilizer.py
import numpy as np

class SwarmStabilizer:
    def __init__(self, lambda_=0.1):
        self.lambda_ = lambda_

    def compute_entropy(self, positions):
        var = np.var(positions, axis=0)
        return np.sum(var)

    def stabilize(self, agents):
        positions = np.array([[a["x"], a["y"]] for a in agents])

        entropy = self.compute_entropy(positions)

        correction = -self.lambda_ * entropy

        for a in agents:
            a["vx"] += correction * 0.01
            a["vy"] += correction * 0.01

        return agents
Effect
prevents collapse
smooths chaotic bursts
keeps swarm “alive but controlled”

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 246: 5. Live Memory + Narrative Drift Tracking (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is what turns Synoptics into a temporal intelligence system.

What you track
system history
behavior drift
narrative shifts over time
Core idea

“how the swarm story is changing”

Implementation
memory_drift.py
import numpy as np
from collections import deque

class NarrativeMemory:
    def __init__(self, size=100):
        self.history = deque(maxlen=size)

    def add_state(self, state):
        self.history.append(state)

    def drift_score(self):
        if len(self.history) < 2:
            return 0

        prev = self.history[-2]
        curr = self.history[-1]

        prev_mean = np.mean([a["x"] for a in prev])
        curr_mean = np.mean([a["x"] for a in curr])

        return abs(curr_mean - prev_mean)
Upgrade version (advanced)

You can plug a transformer later:

state → embedding
embedding drift → narrative shift
