from kafka import KafkaConsumer, KafkaProducer
import json

consumer = KafkaConsumer(
    "swarm.raw.events",
    bootstrap_servers="localhost:9092",
    value_deserializer=lambda x: json.loads(x.decode())
)

producer = KafkaProducer(
    bootstrap_servers="localhost:9092",
    value_serializer=lambda v: json.dumps(v).encode()
)

for msg in consumer:
    state = msg.value

    features = {
        "density": len(state),
        "avg_velocity": sum(a["vx"] for a in state) / len(state)
    }

    producer.send("swarm.features", features)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 252: 3. GPU Inference Layer (HEAVY ML CORE) (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is where your GNN + intent model runs.

Key idea:
Kafka feeds batches
GPU processes batches
outputs predictions + anomalies
Inference Worker
import torch
from kafka import KafkaConsumer, KafkaProducer
import json

model = torch.load("gnn_model.pt")
model.eval()

consumer = KafkaConsumer(
    "swarm.features",
    bootstrap_servers="localhost:9092",
    value_deserializer=lambda x: json.loads(x.decode())
)

producer = KafkaProducer(
    bootstrap_servers="localhost:9092",
    value_serializer=lambda v: json.dumps(v).encode()
)

for msg in consumer:
    features = msg.value

    x = torch.tensor([features["density"], features["avg_velocity"]]).float()

    with torch.no_grad():
        prediction = model(x)

    producer.send("swarm.predictions", {
        "prediction": prediction.tolist()
    })

import WebSocket from "ws";
import Redis from "ioredis";

const wss = new WebSocket.Server({ port: 8080 });
const sub = new Redis();

sub.subscribe("swarm.predictions");

const clients = new Set();

  clients.add(ws);
  ws.on("close", () => clients.delete(ws));
});

sub.on("message", (_, msg) => {
  for (const c of clients) {
    if (c.readyState === 1) {
      c.send(msg);
    }
  }
});

import { Canvas } from "@react-three/fiber";
import { OrbitControls } from "@react-three/drei";
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

function Swarm({ data }) {
  return (
    <>
      {data?.state?.map((a, i) => (
        <mesh key={i} position={[a.x, a.y, a.z || 0]}>
          <sphereGeometry args={[0.2, 16, 16]} />
          <meshStandardMaterial color="lime" />
        </mesh>
      ))}
    </>
  );
}

function PredictionLayer({ data }) {
  return (
    <>
      {data?.prediction?.map((a, i) => (
        <mesh key={i} position={[a.x, a.y, 0]}>
          <sphereGeometry args={[0.15, 16, 16]} />
          <meshStandardMaterial color="cyan" transparent opacity={0.4} />
        </mesh>
      ))}
    </>
  );
}

export default function Synoptics3D() {
  const [data, setData] = useState(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    const ws = new WebSocket("ws://localhost:8080");

    ws.onmessage = (msg) => {
      setData(JSON.parse(msg.data));
    };

    return () => ws.close();
  }, []);

  return (
    <Canvas camera={{ position: [0, 0, 20] }}>
      <ambientLight />
      <OrbitControls />

      <Swarm data={data} />
      <PredictionLayer data={data} />
    </Canvas>
  );
}

   └────────────────────────────┘
Ray Actor Setup (real code)
import ray
import torch

ray.init(address="auto")

@ray.remote(num_gpus=1)
class GNNWorker:
    def __init__(self, model_path):
        self.model = torch.load(model_path)
        self.model.eval()

    def predict(self, batch):
        with torch.no_grad():
            x = torch.tensor(batch).float().cuda()
            return self.model(x).cpu().tolist()
Distributed inference call
workers = [GNNWorker.remote("gnn.pt") for _ in range(4)]

results = ray.get([
    w.predict.remote(batch) for w, batch in zip(workers, batches)
])
What this gives you
horizontal GPU scaling
parallel swarm prediction
near-linear throughput increase

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Section 259: 2. Reinforcement Learning Swarm Control Loop (from *New Agents Swarm  Visualize.txt)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Now your system becomes self-optimizing, not just predictive.

Concept

Agents are no longer just simulated:

RL Loop
state → action → environment → reward → update policy
Swarm Reward Function
def reward(state):
    cohesion = -np.var([a["x"] for a in state])
    stability = -np.std([a["vx"] for a in state])
    efficiency = len(state) / (1 + np.mean([a["energy"] for a in state]))

    return cohesion + stability + efficiency
PPO-style Swarm Agent (simplified)
class SwarmPolicy:
    def act(self, state):
        return {"vx": np.random.randn(), "vy": np.random.randn()}

    def update(self, reward):
        self.learning_rate *= (1 + reward * 0.01)
Result

Swarm begins to:

self-stabilize
self-organize
discover emergent strategies

Causal inference core
def causal_effect(A, B, data):
    do_A = intervene(data, A=1)
    do_not_A = intervene(data, A=0)

    return np.mean(do_A[B]) - np.mean(do_not_A[B])
Explanation generator
def explain_event(event, graph):
    causes = graph.get_parents(event)

    return f"Event occurred due to: {causes}"

1. 🧠 SWARM ENGINE (Python)
services/swarm-engine/main.py
import asyncio
import random
import json
import redis

r = redis.Redis()

AGENTS = [
    {"id": f"a{i}", "x": random.randint(0,100), "y": random.randint(0,100), "vx": 1, "vy": 1}
    for i in range(50)
]

def step():
    for a in AGENTS:
        a["x"] += a["vx"] + random.uniform(-0.5, 0.5)
        a["y"] += a["vy"] + random.uniform(-0.5, 0.5)

    return AGENTS

async def run():
    while True:
        state = step()

        r.set("swarm_state", json.dumps(state))
        r.publish("swarm_stream", json.dumps({
            "type": "state_update",
            "state": state
        }))

        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)

asyncio.run(run())
2. ⚡ NODE WEBSOCKET GATEWAY
apps/api-gateway/server.js
import express from "express";
import http from "http";
import { WebSocketServer } from "ws";
import Redis from "ioredis";

const app = express();
const server = http.createServer(app);
const wss = new WebSocketServer({ server });

const sub = new Redis();

const clients = new Set();

sub.subscribe("swarm_stream");

sub.on("message", (_, msg) => {
  for (const c of clients) {
    if (c.readyState === 1) c.send(msg);
  }
});

wss.on("connection", (ws) => {
  clients.add(ws);
  ws.on("close", () => clients.delete(ws));
});

server.listen(8080, () => {
  console.log("Gateway running on :8080");
});
3. 🧠 SIMPLE “GPU INFERENCE” LAYER (SIMULATED)

(we keep it lightweight for vertical slice)

services/gpu-inference/main.py
import redis
import json
import numpy as np

r = redis.Redis()

def predict(state):
    preds = []
    for a in state:
        preds.append({
            "id": a["id"],
            "x": a["x"] + np.random.randn(),
            "y": a["y"] + np.random.randn(),
            "confidence": float(np.random.rand())
        })
    return preds

pub = r.pubsub()
pub.subscribe("swarm_stream")

for msg in pub.listen():
    if msg["type"] != "message":
        continue

    state = json.loads(msg["data"])

    prediction = predict(state["state"])

    r.publish("swarm_stream", json.dumps({
        "type": "prediction_update",
        "prediction": prediction
    }))
4. 🌐 REACT 3D DASHBOARD (MINIMAL WORKING VERSION)
apps/dashboard/src/App.jsx
import { Canvas } from "@react-three/fiber";
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

function Swarm({ data }) {
  return (
    <>
      {data?.map((a, i) => (
        <mesh key={i} position={[a.x / 10, a.y / 10, 0]}>
          <sphereGeometry args={[0.1, 16, 16]} />
          <meshStandardMaterial color="lime" />
        </mesh>
      ))}
    </>
  );
}

export default function App() {
  const [state, setState] = useState([]);

  useEffect(() => {
    const ws = new WebSocket("ws://localhost:8080");

    ws.onmessage = (msg) => {
      const data = JSON.parse(msg.data);

      if (data.type === "state_update") {
        setState(data.state);
      }
    };

    return () => ws.close();
  }, []);

  return (
    <Canvas camera={{ position: [0, 0, 10] }}>
      <ambientLight />
      <Swarm data={state} />
    </Canvas>
  );
}
